# SF-W2: How do competition law and public procurement bear on AI safety?

Area W · As of clef 7924 Berichte · Luna 7924 Berichte · d3-ultra 4771 Berichte · JEV 5667 Berichte · amos 7924 Berichte · drex 6984 Berichte · SubQ 7924 Berichte

## Subquestions

- [SF-W2-T01](../../fragen/SF-W2/T01/): Which safety-coordination activities between labs need a change or clarification in competition-law treatment, and how is any authorisation confined to exactly those activities, excluding price-setting, customer allocation and exclusion of new entrants?
- [SF-W2-T02](../../fragen/SF-W2/T02/): When the same group of senior officials in a lab assesses both model capabilities and disputed questions about disclosing failures, at what point does aggregating several companies' judgments of this kind about shared classes of incidents become a collusion-adjacent information exchange that would need an antitrust waiver, and who outside the companies can see that boundary approaching?
- [SF-W2-T03](../../fragen/SF-W2/T03/): Which minimum safety criteria, such as re-runnable evaluation cards, sign-off by embedded evaluators or manifests that make any tampering visible, would states make mandatory for every AI system procured with public funds, so that safety commitments attach automatically to these purchases rather than relying on voluntary lab promises?

## Reports by share of levels 4–7

Levels 4–7 / 3–7 = probability each model gives to these levels (3 = useful background or context, 4 = contains some facts that could be cited as evidence … 7 = directly and substantially answers the question). Listed: reports where clef or Luna give levels 3–7 more than 50 %. Sorted by the higher of clef and Luna for levels 4–7. Further models (JEV, amos, d3 …) rated part of the reports; the others show “not yet rated”. The full distribution over the 8 levels is in the data file.
